\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}{\rm diag}\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\bigg)\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\bigg(\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r}{p_{1}}+\frac{\kappa\mu_{0}^{2}r\sqrt{\log(p)}}{\lambda\sqrt{p_{1}}}\bigg)

4.

\|{\rm diag}(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}+\frac{\kappa\mu_{0}^{2}r\sqrt{\log(p)}}{\lambda\sqrt{p_{1}}}

5.

\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{S}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\bigg(\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r}{p_{1}}+\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}}}{\lambda}+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg)^{2}
.

Moreover, all of these terms are upper bounded by the quantity

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}+\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}\bigg)\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}.

Proof of
Lemma

15
.

For part one, we note that

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}_{1}^{-2}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{2,\infty}\frac{\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|\lambda_{1}}{\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\frac{\sqrt{r}\kappa}{\lambda},

since
\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|\lesssim\sqrt{r}
by
Lemma

12
.

For part 2, we note

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\frac{\|\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\|}{\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{\lambda^{2}}.

by
Lemma

12
.

For part 3,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}{\rm diag}\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\bigg)\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\frac{\|{\rm diag}\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\bigg)\|}{\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\max_{i}|e_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{2}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}e_{i}|+\frac{2\|{\rm diag}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top})\|}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\bigg(\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r}{p_{1}}+\frac{2\|{\rm diag}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top})\|}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\bigg(\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r}{p_{1}}+\frac{\kappa\mu_{0}^{2}r\sqrt{\log(p)}}{\lambda\sqrt{p_{1}}}\bigg),

by
Lemma

12
.

For part 4,

\displaystyle\|{\rm diag}(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg(\|{\rm diag}(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top})\|+2\|{\rm diag}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top})\|\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}+\frac{\|{\rm diag}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top})\|}{\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}+\frac{\kappa\mu_{0}^{2}r\sqrt{\log(p)}\|}{\lambda\sqrt{p_{1}}}

by
Lemma

12
.

Finally, by
Lemma

3
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{S}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S},{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\|^{2}.

Note that
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}
are the eigenvectors of
\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}
are the eigenvectors of the matrix

\displaystyle\Gamma\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z_{1}Z_{1}}^{\top}\bigg).

We note that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}-\Gamma\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z_{1}Z_{1}}^{\top}\bigg)\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\rm diag}(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top})\|+2\|\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top})\|+\|\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{1}^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r}{p_{1}}+\lambda_{1}\sqrt{p_{1}}+(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\ll\lambda^{2},

where we note that we used the fact that
\lambda\gtrsim\kappa p_{\max}\sqrt{\log(p)}p_{\min}^{1/4}
, the fact that
\mu_{0}^{2}r\lesssim\sqrt{p_{\min}}
, and the assumption
\kappa\lesssim p_{\min}^{1/4}
. Therefore, by the Davis-Kahan Theorem,

\displaystyle\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S},{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{1}^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r}{p_{1}}+\lambda_{1}\sqrt{p_{1}}+(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r}{p_{1}}+\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}}}{\lambda}+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{\lambda^{2}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{S}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}\bigg(\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r}{p_{1}}+\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}}}{\lambda}+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg)^{2}.

∎

D.5.2
Proof of
Theorem

4

Proof of
Theorem

4
.

Without loss of generality, we consider
k=1
. We simply decompose

\displaystyle\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{S}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{1}^{S}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{S}_{1}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}+\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}{\rm diag}\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\bigg)\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}

\displaystyle\quad-{\rm diag}(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}+{\mathbf{U}}_{1}(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{S}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})

\displaystyle=(I)+(II)+(III)+(IV)+(V)+(VI),

where

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{S}_{1}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1};

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}

\displaystyle(III)

\displaystyle=-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1};

\displaystyle(IV)

\displaystyle=-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}

\displaystyle(V)

\displaystyle={\mathbf{U}}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}{\rm diag}\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\bigg)\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}

\displaystyle(VI)

\displaystyle=-{\rm diag}(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}_{1}

\displaystyle(VII)

\displaystyle={\mathbf{U}}_{1}(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{S}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})

We note that terms
(III)-(VII)
are all of smaller order than the bound we desire by
Lemma

15
(with high probability). With these bounds out of the way, we now turn our attention to terms
(I)
and
(II)
. For Term
(I)
, we simply note that by
Lemma

16

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}_{1}^{-2}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\|\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}\|_{2,\infty}}{\lambda}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\mu_{0}\sqrt{r\log(p)}}{\lambda}.

It remains to show that the final term is of smaller order than the bound we desire, which will require the leave-one-out sequences. Note that

\displaystyle\|e_{m}^{\top}\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\mathbf{\widehat{\Lambda}}^{-2}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|e_{m}^{\top}\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}-\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|\lambda^{-2}+\|e_{m}^{\top}\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|\lambda^{-2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\|\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\|}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}\|+\|e_{m}^{\top}\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|\lambda^{-2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq A+B.

The term
B
: For this term, we note that

\displaystyle e_{m}^{\top}\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\neq m}\langle\mathbf{Z}_{m\cdot},\mathbf{Z}_{a\cdot}\rangle(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})_{a\cdot}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\mathbf{Z}_{ml}\bigg(\sum_{a\neq m}\mathbf{Z}_{al}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})_{a\cdot}\bigg)

is a sum of
p_{-1}
independent random variables (over
l
), and hence satisfies

\displaystyle\|\sum_{l}\mathbf{Z}_{ml}\bigg(\sum_{a\neq m}\mathbf{Z}_{al}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})_{a\cdot}\bigg)\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{p_{-1}\log(p)}\max_{l}\|\sum_{a\neq m}(\mathbf{Z}_{al}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})_{a\cdot}\|.

However
\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}
is still dependent on the
a
’th column of
\mathbf{Z}
, so we introduce a leave-two-out estimator
\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}
, obtained by initializing (with diagonal deletion) with the noise matrix
\mathbf{Z}_{1}
replaced with
\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m,1-l}
. For fixed
l
, we observe that

\displaystyle\|\sum_{a\neq m}(\mathbf{Z}_{al}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})_{a\cdot}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\sum_{a\neq m}(\mathbf{Z}_{al})\big(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})_{a\cdot}-\big(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l})^{\top}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\big)_{a\cdot}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|\sum_{a\neq m}(\mathbf{Z}_{al})\big(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l})^{\top}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\big)_{a\cdot}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathbf{Z}^{-m})^{\top}\|\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l})^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|e_{l}^{\top}(\mathbf{Z}^{-m})^{\top}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|.

Note that by Lemma
17
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|e_{l}^{\top}(\mathbf{Z}^{-m})^{\top}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty}.

(15)

In addition, by the Davis-Kahan Theorem (using the eigengap condition in
Lemma

14
),

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l})^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg(\|\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m,1-l})\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\|\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m})-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m,1-l}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m,1-l})^{\top})\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg(\mu_{0}\lambda_{1}\sqrt{r\log(p)}\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{p_{-1}}}+\sqrt{p_{1}}\log(p)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty}\bigg),

(16)

where the final inequality holds by
Lemma

13
.
Consequently, plugging this and (
15
) into our bound for
B
, we obtain

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{p_{-1}\log(p)}}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg\{\|\mathbf{Z}^{-m}\|\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg(\lambda_{1}\mu_{0}\sqrt{r\log(p)}\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{p_{-1}}}+\sqrt{p_{1}}\log(p)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty}\bigg\}.

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{p_{-1}\log(p)}}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg\{\frac{p}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg(\lambda_{1}\mu_{0}\sqrt{r\log(p)}\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{p_{-1}}}+\sqrt{p_{1}}\log(p)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty}\bigg\},

(17)

where we used the fact that
\|\mathbf{Z}^{-m}\|\leq\|\mathbf{Z}\|\lesssim p
with high probability. The bound (
17
) can be improved so as not to depend on
\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}
. By the bound in (
16
), it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}-\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg(\mu_{0}\lambda_{1}\sqrt{r\log(p)}\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{p_{-1}}}+\sqrt{p_{1}}\log(p)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty}\bigg),

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+o(1)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty},

where we have implicitly observed that

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa p_{1}\sqrt{\log(p)}}{\lambda\sqrt{p_{-1}}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}\leq\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}},

which holds since
\lambda\gtrsim\kappa\sqrt{\log(p)}p/p_{\min}^{1/4}
. By rearranging, we therefore have that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m,1-l}\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}.

Plugging this into (
17
), we obtain

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{p_{-1}\log(p)}}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg\{\frac{p}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg(\lambda_{1}\mu_{0}\sqrt{r\log(p)}\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{p_{-1}}}+\sqrt{p_{1}}\log(p)\|\bigg[\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}\bigg]\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}\bigg[\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}\bigg]\bigg\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{p\sqrt{p_{1}r}\kappa\mu_{0}\log(p)}{\lambda^{3}}+\frac{p\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log^{3/2}(p)}{\lambda^{4}}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{p\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log^{3/2}(p)}{\lambda^{4}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}\bigg(\frac{p\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg)+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}\bigg(1+\frac{p\sqrt{\log(p)}}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}\bigg(1+\frac{p\sqrt{\log(p)}}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}},

which holds whenever
\lambda^{2}\gtrsim p\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}
.
This bound still depends on the leave-one-out sequence, but we will obtain a bound independent of this sequence shortly upon analyzing term
A
. Note that we have also demonstrated the high-probability upper bound

\displaystyle\|e_{m}^{\top}

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}^{S,1-m}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}.

(18)

The term
A
:
Note that by
Lemma

12
we have that
\|\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\|\lesssim(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}
, which yields

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}\|.

Therefore it suffices to bound the term on the right. By the Davis-Kahan Theorem, it holds that

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\|\Gamma((\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m})^{\top}+(\mathbf{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m})\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m})^{\top}\big)\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|}{\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg\{\|\Gamma\big((\mathbf{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m})\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\big)\|+\|\Gamma\big(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m})^{\top}\big)\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|\bigg\}.

By
Lemma

13
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\big((\mathbf{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m})\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\big)\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{1}\mu_{0}\sqrt{r\log(p)},

(19)

so it suffices to consider the second term. Note that the matrix

\displaystyle\Gamma\big(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m})^{\top}\big)

is rank one symmetric matrix whose
(m,l)
entry is simply
\langle e_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1},e_{l}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\rangle
for
l\neq m
. Therefore, define the matrices
\mathbf{G}_{\mathrm{col}}
and
\mathbf{G}_{\mathrm{row}}
with
\mathbf{G}_{\mathrm{col}}
the matrix whose only nonzero entries are in the
m
’th column, in which case they are
\langle e_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1},e_{l}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\rangle
for
l\neq m
, and
\mathbf{G}_{\mathrm{row}}
the matrix whose only nonzero entries are in the
m
’th row, with entries defined similarly. Then

\displaystyle\big\|\Gamma\big(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{1-m})^{\top}\big)\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{G}_{\mathrm{row}}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|+\|\mathbf{G}_{\mathrm{col}}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|.

We consider each term separately. First, note that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{G}_{\mathrm{row}}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|

\displaystyle=\|e_{m}^{\top}\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|.

This was already bounded en route to the analysis for term
B
. In fact, by (
18
) we already have the upper bound

\displaystyle\|e_{m}^{\top}

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}^{S,1-m}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}.

(20)

Next, we argue similarly to the proof of Lemma 4 of
Cai et al. 2021
. We have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{G}_{\mathrm{col}}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{G}_{\mathrm{col}}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{j\neq m}\|\langle e_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1},e_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\rangle(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})_{m\cdot}\|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{j\neq m}|\langle e_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1},e_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\rangle|^{2}\|(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})_{m\cdot}\|^{2}\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}\|\Gamma(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top})\|

\displaystyle\lesssim(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg\{\lambda_{1}\mu_{0}\sqrt{r\log(p)}+\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}.

Therefore, it holds that

\displaystyle\|

\displaystyle\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}-\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p}}+o(1)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty},

so by rearranging we arrive at

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}+\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p}}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}

(21)

Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg\{\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}.

In addition,

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}.

Combining both of these with the initial bounds in
Lemma

15
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\|e_{m}^{\top}\big(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{1}^{S}\big)\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}+\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}\bigg)\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}.

By taking a union bound over all the rows, we have that with probability at least
1-O(p^{-29})
that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}+\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}\bigg)\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|_{2,\infty}+\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+o(1)\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty},

which, by rearranging, yields

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}-{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\mathbf{W}_{1}^{S}\|_{2\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg(\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}+\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}\bigg)\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}.

In addition, (
21
) together with the bound above shows that with high probability,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,1-m})^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r}{p_{1}}}.

Taking another union bound over
m
shows that this bound holds for all
m
with probability at least
1-O(p^{-29})
. Both of these bounds therefore hold with probability at least
1-p^{-20}
.
∎

D.5.3
Proof of
Lemma

2

In this section we prove
Lemma

2
, which controls the remaining two leave-one-out sequences not bounded in
Theorem

4
.

Proof of
Lemma

2
.

First we provide concentration guarantees, similar to
Lemma

13
. We will bound the following terms:

•

\|\Gamma\big(\mathbf{T}_{1}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}\big)\|
;

•

\|\Gamma\big(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}\big)\|

•

\|\Gamma\big(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}\big)\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|
.

First, note that by
Lemma

17
, with probability at least
1-O(p^{-30})
it holds that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\big((\mathbf{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\big)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathbf{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\|+\|{\rm diag}\big((\mathbf{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\big)\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|(\mathbf{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{p_{-j}\log(p)}\|\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{1}\mu_{0}\sqrt{r_{1}\frac{p_{1}}{p_{j}}\log(p)}.

Next, we consider the matrix
\Gamma\big(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}\big)
. First, observe that for
i\neq k
this matrix has entries of the form

\displaystyle\Gamma\big(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}\big)_{ik}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in\Omega}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})_{il}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})_{kl},

where
\Omega
is the set of indices such that the
l
’th column of
\mathbf{Z}_{1}
corresponds to elements belonging to the
m
’th row of
\mathbf{Z}_{j}
. A general formula is possible, but not needed for our purposes here; the cardinality of
\Omega
is equal to the number of nonzero columns of
\mathbf{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m}
, which is
p_{-1-j}
. Since this matrix is a sample gram matrix, by Lemma 1 of
Agterberg et al. 2022b
it holds that with probability at least
1-O(p^{-30})
(where as in the proof of
Lemma

12
the higher probability holds by modifying the constant on
\delta
in the proof of Lemma 1 of
Agterberg et al. 2022b
) that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\big(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}\big)\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{p_{1}}+\sqrt{p_{1}p_{-1-j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{p_{1}}+\sqrt{p_{-j}}

\displaystyle\ll p^{2}/p_{\min}^{1/2}

For the remaining term, we note that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\big(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}\big)\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{p_{1}}\|\Gamma\big(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}\big)\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{p_{1}}\max_{i}\|\sum_{k\neq i,k=1}^{p_{1}}\sum_{l\in\Omega}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})_{il}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})_{kl}\big(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\big)_{k\cdot}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{p_{1}}\sqrt{p_{-1-j}\log(p)}\max_{l}\|\sum_{k\neq i,k=1}^{p_{1}}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})_{kl}\big(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\big)_{k\cdot}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim p_{1}\sqrt{p_{-1-j}}\log(p)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{p_{1}}\sqrt{p_{-j}}\log(p)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|_{2,\infty},

where we have implicitly used the matrix Hoeffding’s inequality twice: once over the summation over
l
conditional on the collection
(\mathbf{Z}_{1})_{kl}
for
k\neq i
, and then again over the summation in
k
.

Note that
\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}
are the eigenvectors of the matrix
\Gamma\big(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\big)
and
\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}
are the eigenvectors of the matrix
\Gamma\big(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\big)
. Therefore, the spectral norm of the difference is upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2\|\Gamma\big(\mathbf{T}_{1}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}\big)\|+\|\Gamma\big(\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}-\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m}(\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{j-m})^{\top}\big)\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{1}\mu_{0}\sqrt{r_{1}\frac{p_{1}}{p_{j}}\log(p)}+p^{2}/p_{\min}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\ll\lambda^{2}.

Moreover,
Lemma

14
shows that

\displaystyle\lambda_{r}\bigg(\Gamma\big(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\big)\bigg)-\lambda_{r+1}\bigg(\Gamma\big(\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathbf{T}_{1}^{\top}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\lambda^{2},

so by the Davis-Kahan Theorem,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{j-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\bigg(\lambda_{1}\mu_{0}\sqrt{r_{1}\frac{p_{1}}{p_{j}}\log(p)}+\sqrt{p_{1}}\sqrt{p_{-j}}\log(p)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|_{2,\infty}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{j}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{-j}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|_{2,\infty}

(22)

In addition, we note that by
Theorem

4
, with probability at least
1-O(p^{-20})
it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|_{2,\infty}+\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}-\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}+\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}+o(1)\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|_{2,\infty},

so by rearranging we obtain that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{1}}}.

Plugging this into (
22
) yields

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{j-m}(\mathbf{\widetilde{U}}_{1}^{S,j-m})^{\top}-\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{1}^{S})^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{j}}}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{-j}}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{r_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{j}}}+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}\log(p)}{\lambda^{2}}\mu_{0}\sqrt{\frac{r_{1}}{p_{j}}}

with probability at least
1-O(p^{-20})
. The proof is then completed by taking a union bound over all
p_{1}
rows.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs of Tensor Mixed-Membership Blockmodel Identifiability and Estimation

In this section we prove our main results concerning the mixed-membership identifiability and estimation. First we establish
Proposition

1
as well as
Lemma

1
relating the properties of the tensor mixed-membership blockmodel to the tensor denoising model. We then prove our estimation guarantees
Theorem

1
. Throughout we let
\mathbf{S}_{k}=\mathcal{M}_{k}(\mathcal{S})
and
\mathbf{T}_{k}
defined similarly.

E.1
Proofs of
Proposition

1
,
Proposition

2
, and
Lemma

1

First we prove
Proposition

2
and
Lemma

1
simultaneously as we will require part of the proof in the proof of
Proposition

1
.

Proof of
Proposition

2
and
Lemma

1
.

For the first part, we follow the proof of Lemma 2.3 of
Mao et al. 2021
. Without loss of generality we prove the result for mode 1. Let
\mathbf{T}_{1}
have singular value decomposition
\mathbf{T}_{1}={\mathbf{U}}_{1}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}
Then since
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{1}\mathbf{S}_{1}(\mathbf{\Pi_{2}}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{3})^{\top},
without loss of generality we may assume the first
r_{1}
rows of
\mathbf{T}_{1}
correspond to pure nodes. We note that therefore

\displaystyle{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{2}({\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{\top}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{S}_{1}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{3})^{\top}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{3})\mathbf{S}_{1}^{\top}.

Since the rank of
\mathbf{\Pi}_{2}
and
\mathbf{\Pi}_{3}
are
r_{2}
and
r_{3}
respectively, it holds that the matrix above is rank
r_{1}
as long as
r_{1}\leq r_{2}r_{3}
since
\mathbf{S}_{1}
is rank
r_{1}
, which shows that
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
is rank
r_{1}
. Furthermore, we have that
\mathbf{T}_{1}[1:r_{1},\cdot]={\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\top}=\mathbf{S}_{1}(\mathbf{\Pi_{2}}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{3})^{\top}
which shows that
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(\mathrm{pure})}=\mathbf{S}_{1}(\mathbf{\Pi_{2}}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{3})^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{-1}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{U}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{T}_{1}\mathbf{V}_{1}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{\Pi}_{1}\mathbf{S}_{1}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{3})^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{1}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{1}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{\Pi}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{(\mathrm{pure})}.

Next, we observe that

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}

\displaystyle=\min_{k}\lambda_{\min}\big(\mathbf{T}_{k}\mathbf{T}_{k}^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle=\min_{k}\lambda_{\min}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}\mathbf{S}_{k}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)^{\top}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\min_{k}\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{\Pi}_{k})\lambda_{\min}\big(\mathbf{S}_{k}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)^{\top}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\min_{k}\frac{p_{k}}{r_{k}}\lambda_{\min}\big(\mathbf{S}_{k}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)^{\top}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\Delta^{2}\min_{k}\frac{p_{k}}{r_{k}}\lambda_{\min}\bigg(\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)^{\top}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\Delta^{2}\frac{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}},

where the penultimate line follows from the fact that
\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)
has full column rank. Therefore,
\lambda\gtrsim\Delta\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3})^{1/2}}
. For the reverse direction, by a similar argument,

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}

\displaystyle=\min_{k}\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{T}_{k}\mathbf{T}_{k}^{\top})

\displaystyle=\min_{k}\lambda_{\min}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}\mathbf{S}_{k}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)^{\top}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{k}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{\Pi}_{k})\lambda_{\min}\big(\mathbf{S}_{k}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)^{\top}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{k}\frac{p_{k}}{r_{k}}\lambda_{\min}\big(\mathbf{S}_{k}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)^{\top}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{k}\frac{p_{k}}{r_{k}}\lambda_{\max}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)^{\top}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+1}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{k+2}\big)\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\min_{k}\frac{p_{k}p_{k+1}p_{k+2}}{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{S}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{S}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq\Delta^{2}\frac{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}},

where we have used the assumption that
\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{\Pi}_{k})\leq\frac{p_{k}}{r_{k}}
.

For the remaining part, we note that by the previous argument, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbf{U}}_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}.

Since
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}=\mathbf{I}_{r_{1}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{\top}\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{\top}={\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{\top},

which demonstrates that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{\top}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{\Pi}_{k})^{-1}.

Since
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
is an
r_{1}\times r_{1}
matrix and
\lambda_{r_{1}}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{\Pi}_{k})\gtrsim\frac{p_{k}}{r_{k}}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle=\max_{i}\langle\big({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\big)_{i\cdot},\big({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\big)_{i\cdot}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{\top}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}.

Since
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
has rows that are convex combinations of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{r_{k}}{p_{k}}},

which demonstrates that
\mu_{0}=O(1)
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

1
.

The proof of the first part is similar to Theorem 2.1 of
Mao et al. 2021
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{T}_{k}
has SVD
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}\mathbf{\Lambda}_{k}\mathbf{V}_{k}^{\top}
. By
Proposition

2
(which only relies on the assumptions in
Proposition

1
) it holds that there an invertible matrix
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
such that
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
, where
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
consists of the rows of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}
corresponding to pure nodes. Therefore, for each
i
it holds that
({\mathbf{U}}_{k})_{i\cdot}
is in the convex hull of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
.

Now suppose that there exists other parameters
\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathbf{\Pi}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{\Pi}_{2}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{\Pi}_{3}^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\times_{1}\mathbf{\Pi}_{1}^{\prime}\times_{2}\mathbf{\Pi}_{2}^{\prime}\times_{3}\mathbf{\Pi}_{3}^{\prime}
, where each
\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\prime}
may have different pure nodes. Note that since
\mathcal{T}
is the same regardless of
\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}
and
\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\prime}
, its singular value decomposition is fixed (where we arbitrarily specify a choice of sign for unique singular values or basis for repeated singular values). By the previous argument we have that
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
must belong to the convex hull of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
, where
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
corresponds to the pure nodes associated to
\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\prime}
. By applying
Proposition

2
again to the new decomposition, it must hold that
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\prime}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
, which shows that
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
belongs to the convex hull of
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
. Since both convex hulls are subsets of each other, it holds that the convex hulls of
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
and
\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
are the same. Consequently, it must hold that
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}=\mathcal{P}_{k}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
for some permutation matrix
\mathcal{P}_{k}
.

Now we note that by the identity
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\prime}\widetilde{{\mathbf{U}}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
, it holds that
\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\prime}\mathcal{P}_{k}{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
, which demonstrates that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}-\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\prime}\mathcal{P}_{k}){\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}=0.

Since
{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
is full rank, it must therefore hold that
\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}=\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}^{\prime}\mathcal{P}_{k}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\times_{1}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{1}\mathcal{P}_{1}\big)\times_{2}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{2}\mathcal{P}_{2}\big)\times_{3}\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{3}\mathcal{P}_{3}\big),

which shows that
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\times_{1}\mathcal{P}_{1}\times_{2}\mathcal{P}_{2}\times_{3}\mathcal{P}_{3}
, which completes the proof of the first part of the result.

The second part of the result essentially follows the proof of Theorem 2.2 of
Mao et al. 2021
. Without loss of generality we prove the result for mode
1
. Assume for contradiction that there is a community without any pure nodes; without loss of generality let it be the first community. Then there is some
\delta>0
such that
(\mathbf{\Pi}_{1})_{i1}\leq 1-\delta
for all
i
. Define

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}:

\displaystyle=\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}1+(r_{1}-1)\varepsilon^{2}&-\varepsilon^{2}\mathbf{1}^{\top}_{r_{1}-1}\\
\hline\cr 0&\varepsilon\mathbf{1}_{r_{1}-1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}_{r_{1}-1}+(1-(r_{1}-1)\varepsilon)\mathbf{I}_{r_{1}-1}\end{array}\right],

where
0<\varepsilon<\delta
. For
\varepsilon
sufficiently small,
\mathbf{H}
is full rank, and the rows of
\mathbf{H}
sum to one. Consequently,
\mathbf{\widetilde{\Pi}}_{1}:=\mathbf{\Pi}_{1}\mathbf{H}
also has rows that sum to one. Moreover, for any
i
,
(\mathbf{\widetilde{\Pi}}_{1})_{i1}=(\mathbf{\Pi}_{1})_{i1}(1+(K-1)\varepsilon^{2})\geq 0
, and for any
2\leq l\leq r_{1}
,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{\widetilde{\Pi}}_{1})_{il}

\displaystyle=-(\mathbf{\Pi}_{1})_{i1}\varepsilon^{2}+\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{r_{1}}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{1})_{il^{\prime}}\mathbf{H}_{l^{\prime}l}

\displaystyle=-(\mathbf{\Pi}_{1})_{i1}\varepsilon^{2}+(\mathbf{\Pi}_{1})_{il}(1-(K-1)\varepsilon)+\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{r_{1}}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{1})_{il^{\prime}}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\geq-(\mathbf{\Pi}_{1})_{i1}\varepsilon^{2}+\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{r_{1}}(\mathbf{\Pi}_{1})_{il^{\prime}}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\geq(1-\delta)\varepsilon^{2}+\varepsilon\delta

\displaystyle>0,

and hence
\mathbf{\widetilde{\Pi}}_{1}
has positive entries. Therefore, for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small
\mathbf{\widetilde{\Pi}}_{1}
is a valid membership matrix. In addition, we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{T})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{\Pi}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{S})\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{3}\big)^{\top}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{\widetilde{\Pi}}_{1}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{S})\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{3}\big)^{\top}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{\widetilde{\Pi}}_{1}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathcal{S}\times_{1}\mathbf{H}^{-1})\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{\Pi}_{3}\big)^{\top},

which shows that
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{\widetilde{S}}\times_{1}\mathbf{\widetilde{\Pi}}_{1}\times_{2}\mathbf{\Pi}_{2}\times_{3}\mathbf{\Pi}_{3}
is another representation of
\mathcal{T}
, where
\mathcal{\widetilde{S}}=\mathcal{S}\times_{1}\mathbf{H}^{-1}
. Since
\mathbf{H}
is not a permutation matrix, we see that we have a contradiction, which completes the proof.
∎

E.2
Proof of
Theorem

1

Proof of
Theorem

1
.

Our proof is similar to the proof of the main result in
Mao et al. 2021
as well as the proof of Theorem 4.9 in
Xie 2022
, where we will apply Theorem 3 of
Gillis and Vavasis 2014
. We first prove the result assuming that
\lambda/\sigma\gtrsim\kappa p\sqrt{\log(p)}/p_{\min}^{1/4}
, that
r\leq p_{\min}^{1/4}
and that
\mu_{0}=O(1)
; the result will then follow by applying
Lemma

1
. Without loss of generality, we prove the result for
k=1
.

First, observe that by
Theorem

2
, with probability at least
1-p^{-10}
it holds that there is an orthogonal matrix
\mathbf{W}
such that for
t
iterations with
t
as in
Theorem

2
it holds that the output
\mathbf{\widehat{U}}
of HOOI satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbf{\widehat{U}}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{U}}\mathbf{W}+\mathrm{error},

with

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{error}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{r_{k}\log(p)}}{\lambda/\sigma}.

Since by
Lemma

1

{\mathbf{U}}=\mathbf{\Pi}{\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{W}^{\top}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{\top}\mathbf{\Pi}^{\top}+\mathrm{error}^{\top}.

We will apply Theorem 3 of
Gillis and Vavasis 2014
, with
\mathbf{M}
,
\mathbf{W}
,
\mathbf{H}
and
\mathbf{N}
therein equal to
{\mathbf{U}}
,
\mathbf{W}^{\top}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{\top}
,
\mathbf{\Pi}^{\top}
and
\mathrm{error}^{\top}
respectively. Define, for some sufficiently large constant
C
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon

\displaystyle\coloneqq C\frac{\kappa\sqrt{r_{k}\log(p)}}{\lambda/\sigma}.

It then holds that
\|\mathrm{error}_{i\cdot}\|\leq\varepsilon
on the event in
Theorem

2
. We also need to check the bound

\displaystyle\varepsilon<\lambda_{\min}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})\min\bigg(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{r_{1}-1}},\frac{1}{4}\bigg)\bigg(1+80\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})}{\sigma_{r}^{2}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})}\bigg)^{-1}.

First we note that by the proof of
Lemma

1
, we have that
\lambda_{\min}^{2}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})=\lambda_{\min}\big({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{\top}\big)=\lambda_{\min}\big((\mathbf{\Pi^{\top}\Pi})^{-1}\big)
. Since
\lambda_{\max}\big(\mathbf{\Pi^{\top}\Pi}\big)\lesssim\frac{p_{1}}{r_{1}}
, we have that
\lambda_{\min}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})\gtrsim\frac{\sqrt{r_{1}}}{\sqrt{p_{1}}}
.

We note that

\displaystyle\frac{\|{\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}}{\lambda_{r}^{2}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})}{\lambda_{r}^{2}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})}{\lambda_{r}^{2}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Pi^{\top}\Pi})}{\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Pi^{\top}\Pi})}

\displaystyle\asymp C,

since
\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Pi^{\top}\Pi})\gtrsim p_{1}/r_{1}
by assumption. Consequently, plugging in these estimates, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\varepsilon<c\frac{\sqrt{r_{1}}}{\sqrt{p_{1}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{r_{1}}}=\frac{c}{\sqrt{p_{1}}},

where
c
is some sufficiently small constant. Plugging in the definition of
\varepsilon
, we see that we require that

\displaystyle C\frac{\kappa\sqrt{r_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda/\sigma}\leq\frac{c}{\sqrt{p_{1}}},

which is equivalent to the condition

\displaystyle\lambda/\sigma\gtrsim\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}r_{1}\log(p)},

which holds under the condition
\lambda/\sigma\gtrsim\kappa p\sqrt{\log(p)}/p_{\min}^{1/4}
and
r\leq p_{\min}^{1/4}
. Therefore, we may apply Theorem 3 of
Gillis and Vavasis 2014
to find that there exists a permutation
\mathcal{P}
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}-\mathcal{P}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W}\|_{2,\infty}\leq C\varepsilon.

We now use this bound to provide our final bound.
First, since
\varepsilon\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}
, by Weyl’s inequality it holds that

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{\min}({\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{(\mathrm{pure})})-\sqrt{r_{1}}\varepsilon

\displaystyle\geq C\frac{\sqrt{r_{1}}}{\sqrt{p_{1}}}-\frac{c\sqrt{r_{1}}}{\sqrt{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\sqrt{r_{1}}}{{\sqrt{p_{1}}}},

as long as
c
is sufficiently small. Consequently,
\|(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}\|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{r_{1}}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widehat{\Pi}}-\mathbf{\Pi}\mathcal{P}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}-{\mathbf{U}}({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}\mathcal{P}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}-{\mathbf{U}}\mathbf{W}(\mathcal{P}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W})^{-1}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\big(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}-{\mathbf{U}}\mathbf{W}\big)(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}\|_{2,\infty}+\|{\mathbf{U}}\mathbf{W}\big((\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}-(\mathcal{P}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W})^{-1}\big)\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{\widehat{U}}-{\mathbf{U}}\mathbf{W}\|_{2,\infty}\|(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}\|+\|\mathbf{\Pi}{\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W}\big((\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}-(\mathcal{P}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W})^{-1}\big)\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\|(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}\|+\|\mathbf{\Pi}\|_{\infty\to\infty}\|{\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W}\big((\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}-(\mathcal{P}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W})^{-1}\big)\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\|(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}\|+\|{\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W}\big((\mathcal{P}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}-({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W})^{-1}\big)\mathcal{P}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\|(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}\|+\|\big({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W}(\mathcal{P}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}-\mathbf{I}_{r_{k}}\big)\mathcal{P}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\|(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}\|+\|\big({\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W}-\mathcal{P}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\big)(\mathcal{P}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}\mathcal{P}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\|(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}\|+\|\mathcal{P}^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\mathbf{W}-\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})}\|_{2,\infty}\|(\mathcal{P}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(\mathrm{pure})})^{-1}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\sqrt{p_{1}/r_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sqrt{r_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda/\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{p_{1}}{r_{1}}}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda/\sigma}.

Therefore, all that remains is to apply
Lemma

1
. First, we need to check that the condition

\displaystyle\lambda/\sigma\gtrsim\kappa p\sqrt{\log(p)}/p_{\min}^{1/4}

holds; by
Lemma

1
this is equivalent to the condition

\displaystyle\Delta/\sigma\gtrsim\frac{\kappa p\sqrt{\log(p)}}{p_{\min}^{1/4}}\frac{\sqrt{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}}}{\sqrt{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}},

which is in Assumption
2
.
Similarly, the upper bound on the number of iterations applies by substituting
\Delta/\sigma\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3})^{1/2}}
for the quantity
\lambda/\sigma
and adjusting the constant
c
in the exponential.
Finally, by
Lemma

1
, we obtain the final upper bound

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\widehat{\Pi}}-\mathbf{\Pi}\mathcal{P}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\kappa\sigma\sqrt{r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}\log(p)}}{\Delta(p_{-1})^{1/2}},

as desired.
∎

E.3
Proof of
Corollary

1

Proof of
Corollary

1
.

Fix an index
k
, and let
\mathcal{P}
denote the permutation matrix from
Theorem

1
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathrm{Permutations}\ \mathcal{P}}\|\big(\mathbf{\widehat{\Pi}}_{k}-\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}\mathcal{P}\big)_{i\cdot})_{i\cdot}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{r_{k}}\|\big(\mathbf{\widehat{\Pi}}_{k}-\mathbf{\Pi}_{k}\mathcal{P}\big)_{i\cdot}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r^{2}\kappa\sqrt{\log(p)}}{(\Delta/\sigma)(p_{-k})^{1/2}}.

Averaging over the rows completes the proof.
∎

Appendix F
Auxiliary Probabilistic Lemmas

Lemma 16
.

Let
\mathbf{A}
be any fixed matrix independent from
e_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{k}
. Then there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that with probability at least
1-O(p_{\max}^{-20})
,

\displaystyle\|e_{m}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{k}\mathbf{A}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma\sqrt{p_{-k}\log(p_{\max})}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2,\infty}.

Proof.

This follows from
Cai et al. 2021
, Lemma 12.
∎

Lemma 17
.

Let
\mathbf{A}
be a matrix independent from
\mathbf{Z}_{k}-\mathbf{Z}_{k}^{j-m}
, where
\mathbf{Z}_{k}^{j-m}
is defined in
Appendix

D
. Then there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that with probability at least
1-O(p_{\max}^{-30})
,

\displaystyle\|\bigg(\mathbf{Z}_{k}-\mathbf{Z}_{k}^{j-m}\bigg)\mathbf{A}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma\sqrt{p_{-j}\log(p_{\max})}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2,\infty}.

Proof.

If
j=k
, the result follows by Lemma
16
. Therefore, we restrict our attention to when
j\neq k
. First, note that

\displaystyle\|\bigg(\mathbf{Z}_{k}-\mathbf{Z}_{k}^{j-m}\bigg)\mathbf{A}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{p_{k}}\|\bigg(\mathbf{Z}_{k}-\mathbf{Z}_{k}^{j-m}\bigg)\mathbf{A}\|_{2,\infty}.

Next, consider any fixed row of
\mathbf{Z}_{k}-\mathbf{Z}_{k}^{j-m}
. Observe that the
q
’th row can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{\Omega}\big(\mathbf{Z}_{k}\big)_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot},

where the set
\Omega
consists of the
p_{-k-j}
random variables in the
q
’th row of
\mathbf{Z}_{k}-\mathbf{Z}_{k}^{j-m}
. Note that this is a sum of independent random matrices. By the matrix Bernstein inequality (Proposition 2 of
Koltchinskii et al. 2011
), it holds that with probability at least
1-p_{\max}^{-31}
that

\displaystyle\|\sum_{\Omega}\big(\mathbf{Z}_{k}\big)_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\max\bigg\{\sigma_{Z}\sqrt{p_{-k-j}\log(p_{\max})},U_{Z}\log(p)\bigg\}

where

\displaystyle\sigma_{Z}^{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\max_{l}\max\bigg\{\bigg\|\mathbb{E}\big(\big(\mathbf{Z}_{k}\big)_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\big)\big(\big(\mathbf{Z}_{k}\big)_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\big)^{\top}\bigg\|,\bigg\|\mathbb{E}\big(\big(\mathbf{Z}_{k}\big)_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\big)^{\top}\big(\big(\mathbf{Z}_{k}\big)_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\big)\bigg\|\bigg\};

\displaystyle U_{Z}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\max_{l}\|(\mathbf{Z}_{k})_{ql}A_{l\cdot}\|_{\psi_{2}}

(Note that Proposition 2 of
Koltchinskii et al. 2011
holds for IID random matrices, but the proof works equally as well if uniform bounds on
\sigma_{Z}
and
U_{Z}
are obtained). Observe that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\mathbb{E}\bigg[(\mathbf{Z}_{k})_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\bigg]\bigg[(\mathbf{Z}_{k})_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\bigg]^{\top}\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}\|\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2,\infty}^{2};

\displaystyle\bigg\|\mathbb{E}\bigg[(\mathbf{Z}_{k})_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\bigg]^{\top}\bigg[(\mathbf{Z}_{k})_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\bigg]\bigg\|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}\ \|\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}.

Similarly, by subgaussianity of the entries of
\mathbf{Z}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\max_{l}\|(\mathbf{Z}_{k})_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\|_{\psi_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2,\infty}.

Therefore, with probability at least
1-p_{\max}^{-31}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\sum_{\Omega}\big(\mathbf{Z}_{k}\big)_{ql}\mathbf{A}_{l\cdot}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\max\bigg\{\sigma_{Z}\sqrt{p_{-k-j}\log(p_{\max})},U_{Z}\log(p_{\max})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2,\infty}\max\bigg\{\sqrt{p_{-k-j}\log(p_{\max})},\log(p_{\max})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2,\infty}\sqrt{p_{-k-j}\log(p_{\max})}.

Taking a union bound over all
p_{k}
rows shows that this holds uniformly with probability at least
1-O(p_{\max}^{-30})
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg(\mathbf{Z}_{k}-\mathbf{Z}_{k}^{j-m}\bigg)\mathbf{A}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{p_{k}}\|\bigg(\mathbf{Z}_{k}-\mathbf{Z}_{k}^{j-m}\bigg)\mathbf{A}\bigg\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma\|\mathbf{A}\|_{2,\infty}\sqrt{p_{-j}\log(p_{\max})}

as desired.
∎

Lemma 18
.

Suppose
\mathcal{Z}\in{\mathbf{R}}^{p_{1}\times p_{2}\times p_{3}}
is a tensor with mean-zero subgaussian entries, each with
\psi_{2}
norm bounded by
1
. Suppose that
r_{2}r_{3}\leq p_{1}r_{1}
. Then for some universal constant
C
, the following holds with probability at least
1-c\exp(-cp_{\max})
:

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\|=1,\mathrm{rank}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})\leq 2r_{1}\\
,\|{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\|=1,\mathrm{rank}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})\leq 2r_{2}\end{subarray}}\|\mathbf{Z}\bigg(\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{2}}\bigg)\|

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{p_{\max}r_{\max}}.

Proof.

See Lemma 8 of
Han et al. 2022
or Lemma 3 of
Zhang and Han 2019
.
∎

Lemma 19
.

Define
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Good}}
as in (
12
). Then under the conditions of
Theorem

2
, it holds that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Good}})\geq 1-O(p^{-30})
.

Proof.

Recall the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Good}}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{Good}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigg\{\max_{k}\tau_{k}\leq C\sqrt{pr}\bigg\}\bigcap\bigg\{\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{k}^{(t)},{\mathbf{U}}_{k})\|\leq\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{L}}^{(k)}}{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2^{t}}\text{ for all $t\leq t_{\max}$ and $1\leq k\leq 3$ }\bigg\}

\displaystyle\qquad\bigcap\bigg\{\max_{k}\bigg\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{k}\mathbf{V}_{k}\bigg\|\leq C\left(\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{\log(p)}\right)\bigg\};

\displaystyle\qquad\bigcap\bigg\{\max_{k}\bigg\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{k}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}}\bigg\|\leq C\left(r+\sqrt{\log(p)}\right)\bigg\};

\displaystyle\qquad\bigcap\bigg\{\max_{k}\bigg\|\mathbf{Z}_{k}\mathbf{V}_{k}\bigg\|\leq C\sqrt{p_{k}}\bigg\}.

(23)

Define the events

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}:

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\max_{k}\tau_{k}\leq C\sqrt{pr}\bigg\};

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}:

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{k}^{(t)},{\mathbf{U}}_{k})\|\leq\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{L}}^{(k)}}{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2^{t}}\text{ for all $t\leq t_{\max}$ and $1\leq k\leq 3$ }\bigg\};

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{3}:

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\max_{k}\bigg\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{k}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k+1}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{k+2}}\bigg\|\leq C\left(r+\sqrt{\log(p)}\right)\bigg\};

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}:

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\max_{k}\bigg\|{\mathbf{U}}_{k}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{k}\mathbf{V}_{k}\bigg\|\leq C\left(\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{\log(p)}\right)\bigg\};

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{5}:

\displaystyle=\bigg\{\max_{k}\bigg\|\mathbf{Z}_{k}\mathbf{V}_{k}\bigg\|\leq C\sqrt{p_{k}}\bigg\}.

We aim to demonstrate that each event
\mathcal{E}_{i}
holds with probability at least
1-O(p^{-30})
under the conditions of
Theorem

2
, whence the result is complete via a union bound. First we will verify all events except
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, which we will do last.

•

The event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
: We recall that
\tau_{k}
is defined via

\displaystyle\tau_{k}:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|\mathbf{U}_{1}\|=1,\mathrm{rank}({\mathbf{U}}_{1})\leq 2r_{k+1}\\
\|\mathbf{U}_{2}\|=1,\mathrm{rank}({\mathbf{U}}_{2})\leq 2r_{k+2}\end{subarray}}\|\mathbf{Z}_{k}\bigg(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}}\bigg)\|.

Therefore, the result is implied by
Lemma

18
.

•

The event
\mathcal{E}_{3}
:
We note that it suffices to prove the bound for
k=1
since the right hand side is invariant to the index
k
. First, we note that by properties of the Kronecker product and projection matrices,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}_{2}}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{U}_{3}}\|

\displaystyle=\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)\|.

Next, let
x
and
y
be deterministic unit vectors of dimensions
r_{1}
and
r_{2}r_{3}
respectively. Observe that

\displaystyle x^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)y=\sum_{i=1}^{p_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p_{2}p_{3}}({\mathbf{U}}_{1}x)_{i}(\mathbf{Z}_{1})_{ij}\bigg(\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)y\bigg)_{j},

which is a sum of independent random variables. By Hoeffding’s inequality for subgaussian random variables, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\big|x^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)y\big|\geq t\bigg\}\leq 2\exp\bigg\{-c\frac{t^{2}}{\|\mathbf{U}_{1}x\|^{2}\|\mathbf{U}_{2}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{3}y\|^{2}}\bigg\}.

By taking
t=C(r+\sqrt{\log(p)})\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}x\|\|{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}y\|
, we have that

\displaystyle\big|x^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)y\big|

\displaystyle\leq C(r+\sqrt{\log(p)})\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}x\|\|{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}y\|

\displaystyle\leq C(r+\sqrt{\log(p)})

with probability at least
1-2\exp\bigg(-C(r+\sqrt{\log(p)})^{2}\bigg)
, where we have used the fact that
{\mathbf{U}}_{1}
and
{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}
are orthonormal matrices. Since the bound above does not depend on
x
and
y
, let
\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
\mathcal{N}_{2}
be
1/4
-nets for the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}}

\mathbb{R}^{r_{2}r_{3}}
respectively. By Corollary 4.2.13 of
Vershynin 2018
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{N}_{1}|\leq 12^{r_{1}}\qquad|\mathcal{N}_{2}|\leq 12^{r_{2}r_{3}}.

Therefore, by taking a union bound, we see that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathcal{N}_{1},y\in\mathcal{N}_{2}}\big|x^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)y\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\big(r+\sqrt{\log(p)}\big)

with probability at least
1-12^{r_{1}+r_{2}r_{3}}\exp\bigg(-C(r+\sqrt{\log(p)})^{2}\bigg)\geq 1-O(p^{-30})
as long as
C
is chosen sufficiently large. Finally, by exercise 4.4.3 of
Vershynin 2018
it holds that

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|x\|=1,\|y\|=1}|\big|x^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)y\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{x\in\mathcal{N}_{1},y\in\mathcal{N}_{2}}\big|x^{\top}{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{Z}_{1}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\big)y\big|.

This shows that
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{E}_{3}\big)\geq 1-O(p^{-30})
as required.

•

The events
\mathcal{E}_{4}
and
\mathcal{E}_{5}
:
These bounds follow from the same arguments as the previous bound, only taking
1/4
nets over different dimensions.

•

The event
\mathcal{E}_{2}
: We will replicate the proof of Theorem 1 of
Zhang and Xia 2018
. First, we note that by the proof of
Lemma

14
(which does not depend on the statement of this result), it holds that the initialization satisfies

\displaystyle\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{k}^{(0)},{\mathbf{U}}_{k})\|\leq\kappa^{2}\mu_{0}^{2}\frac{r}{p_{k}}+\frac{\kappa\sqrt{p_{k}}}{\lambda}+\frac{(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{1/2}}{\lambda^{2}},

which together with the assumption
\lambda\gtrsim\kappa\sqrt{\log(p)}p/p_{\min}^{1/4}
implies that for each
k
,

\displaystyle\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{k}^{(0)},{\mathbf{U}}_{k})\|\leq\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{C_{0}\kappa\sqrt{p_{k}\log(p)}}{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}.

(24)

These bounds hold with probability at least
1-O(p_{\max}^{-30})
. Let
\mathcal{E}
be the event that (
24
) holds and that
\mathcal{E}_{1}
holds. We will show that deterministically on this event that our required bound holds. The proof is by induction on
t
. Suppose the bound holds up to some
t
. Define

\displaystyle\mathbf{\widehat{T}}_{1}^{(t+1)}:=\mathcal{M}_{1}\big(\mathcal{\widehat{T}}\times_{2}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(t)})^{\top}\times_{3}({\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{(t)})^{\top}\big);

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{1}^{(t)}:=\mathcal{M}_{1}\big(\mathcal{T}\times_{2}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(t)})^{\top}\times_{3}({\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{(t)})^{\top}\big);

\displaystyle\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{(t)}:=\mathcal{M}_{1}\big(\mathcal{Z}\times_{2}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{(t)})^{\top}\times_{3}({\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{(t)})^{\top}\big).

It holds that

\displaystyle\lambda_{r_{1}}\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}^{(t+1)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r_{1}}\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)}\otimes\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{3}^{(t)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{r_{1}}\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})^{\top}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)}\otimes\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{3}^{(t)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{r_{1}}\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\bigg)\lambda_{\min}\bigg(({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})^{\top}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)}\otimes\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{3}^{(t)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{r_{1}}\bigg(\mathbf{T}_{1}{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3}\bigg)\lambda_{\min}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)}\big)\lambda_{\min}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{3}^{(t)}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda(1-\frac{1}{4}),

where we have used the fact that
\lambda_{\min}({\mathbf{U}}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)})=\sqrt{1-\|\sin\Theta({\mathbf{U}}_{2},\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)})\|^{2}}
. Furthermore, we have that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Z}_{1}^{(t)}\|

\displaystyle=\|\mathbf{Z}_{1}(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)}\otimes\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{3}^{(t)}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{Z}_{1}\big({\mathbf{U}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\|+\|\mathbf{Z}_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{2\perp}}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{3}}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{3}^{(t)}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\|\mathbf{Z}_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{2}}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{3\perp}}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{3}^{(t)}\|+\|\mathbf{Z}_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{2\perp}}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\mathbf{U}}_{3\perp}}\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{3}^{(t)}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{p_{1}}+3C\sqrt{pr}\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)},{\mathbf{U}}_{2})\|+C\sqrt{pr}\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{3}^{(t)},{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\|

\displaystyle\quad+C\sqrt{pr}\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{2}^{(t)},{\mathbf{U}}_{2})\|\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{3}^{(t)},{\mathbf{U}}_{3})\|

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{p_{1}}+3C\sqrt{pr}\bigg(\frac{\delta^{(2)}_{L}}{\lambda}+\frac{\delta^{(3)}_{L}}{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2^{t}}\bigg).

therefore, since
\mathbf{T}_{1}^{(t)}
and
\mathbf{T}_{1}
have the same left singular vectors, Wedin’s Theorem implies that

\displaystyle\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}^{(t+1)}_{1},{\mathbf{U}}_{1})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\sqrt{p_{1}}}{\lambda}+\frac{3C\sqrt{pr}}{\lambda}\bigg(\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{L}}^{(2)}}{\lambda}+\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{L}}^{(3)}}{\lambda}\bigg)+\frac{3C\sqrt{pr}}{\lambda}\frac{1}{2^{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\sqrt{p_{1}}}{\lambda}+\frac{3CC_{0}\kappa\sqrt{pp_{2}r\log(p)}}{\lambda}+\frac{3CC_{0}\kappa\sqrt{pp_{3}r\log(p)}}{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}\kappa\sqrt{p_{1}\log(p)}}{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2^{t+1}},

where the final inequality holds from the assumption that
\lambda\gtrsim\kappa\sqrt{\log(p)}p/p_{\min}^{1/4}
. Arguing similarly for the other modes, we see that for all
t
and
k
it holds that

\displaystyle\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{\widehat{U}}_{k}^{(t)},{\mathbf{U}}_{k})\|\leq\frac{\delta_{\mathrm{L}}^{(k)}}{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2^{t}}

with probability at least
1-O(p^{-30})
, which completes the proof.

∎
